## 0.30.0

This version of Klive is a significant update with an entirely new architecture. It is not just about new features and bug fixes but a rethought, brand-new Klive IDE. These are the essential changes:

- It is entirely written in TypeScript, with no WebAssembly anymore.
- The build is based on Vite.
- The documentation engine has been changed from Jekyll to Nextra.

2c:["$","ul",null,{"className":"x:[:is(ol,ul)_&]:my-[.75em] x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:list-disc x:ms-[1.5em]","children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Execution History on every Z80 machine:"}]," ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Debug › Execution History"}]," (or ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"show-history"}],")\nlists the last instructions the CPU ran in a debug session: 131,072 on the Next and 65,536 on the\nother machines. Each row shows the bank and address, the bytes that actually ran, the disassembly,\nthe source line, and the registers and flags the instruction changed. A HALT wait, a ZX80/ZX81\ndisplay line and a Next DMA bus hold are each one row with a count, and ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Fold interrupts"}]," turns\neach interrupt service into one expandable row. Click a row for the full registers before and\nafter it and the memory map at that time; double-click it to go to its source. A plain ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Start"}],"\nrecords nothing (#1445)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Reverse debugging on every Z80 machine:"}]," while a debug session is paused, ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Step Back"}],", ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Step\nForward"}],", ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Reverse Step Over"}],", ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Reverse Step Out"}]," and ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Reverse Continue"}]," (Alt+F5) put the\nwhole machine back at any recorded instruction, memory, devices and screen included, and every\npanel shows that moment. Stepping or continuing from the past replays the recorded run with your\nbreakpoints active, and ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Take over here"}]," makes the current point the present and goes on live\n(on the Next this undoes the SD card writes of the discarded future). Reverse Continue checks\nevery breakpoint kind, so a memory-write breakpoint finds where a byte last changed; a long search\nshows its progress and can be cancelled (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"reverse-continue-cancel"}],"). The status bar shows how far\nback you are, and a click returns to the present. ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Settings › Debugging"}]," sets how much memory\nthe timeline may use (#1445)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Trace export:"}]," ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Debug › Export Execution History…"}],", the Execution History’s ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Export"}],"\nbutton or ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"history-export"}]," (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"hexp"}],") saves a range of the history as a text or CSV trace. The\ndefaults suit diffing two runs: times are relative to the first row, the header has no export\ntime, and ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"-nointerrupts"}]," leaves interrupt services out. The columns can be chosen, and CSV has\none column per register (#1445)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Debug recordings:"}]," ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Debug › Save Debug Recording…"}]," (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"drsave"}],") saves a reverse-debugging\nsession to a ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":".klr"}]," file. ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Debug › Open Debug Recording…"}]," (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"drload"}],"), ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"File › Open File…"}],"\nor dropping the file on the Emulator window opens it on the same machine and configuration, paused\nwhere it was saved, with its whole past to step back through; its breakpoints and watches come\nwith it as session breakpoints. A minute of a ZX Spectrum game takes about 1-2 MB, and ","$L150","\nfiles have a viewer in the Explorer. A recording replays only in the Klive build that made it; for\na file from another build, Klive offers to open its end state instead (#1445)."]}],"\n","$L151","\n","$L152","\n","$L153","\n","$L154","\n","$L155","\n","$L156","\n","$L157","\n","$L158","\n","$L159","\n","$L15a","\n","$L15b","\n","$L15c","\n","$L15d","\n","$L15e","\n","$L15f","\n","$L160","\n","$L161","\n","$L162","\n","$L163","\n","$L164","\n","$L165","\n","$L166","\n","$L167","\n","$L168","\n","$L169","\n","$L16a","\n","$L16b","\n","$L16c","\n","$L16d","\n","$L16e","\n","$L16f","\n","$L170","\n","$L171","\n","$L172","\n","$L173","\n","$L174","\n","$L175","\n","$L176","\n","$L177","\n","$L178","\n","$L179","\n","$L17a","\n","$L17b","\n","$L17c","\n","$L17d","\n","$L17e","\n","$L17f","\n","$L180","\n","$L181","\n","$L182","\n"]}]

2e:["$","ul",null,{"className":"x:[:is(ol,ul)_&]:my-[.75em] x:not-first:mt-[1.25em] x:list-disc x:ms-[1.5em]","children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":"Cambridge Z88: on the 640x256, 640x320 and 640x480 LCDs, a small Screen Base File (such as OZ\nV5.0B’s 2K one) painted unrelated memory as noise below its last row. They\nnow follow the memory the CPU actually reads (#1445)."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":["ZX Spectrum 128K, Pentagon, Scorpion and +2A/+3/+3E: after a project with ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":".bank"}]," segments was\nstarted, the Memory, Disassembly, Watch and System Variables views showed the old bytes of a\npaged-in bank until the next paging change or reset. 1bb:["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Step Out could run away instead of stopping"}],", on every Z80 machine. The debugger’s shadow stack\nof return addresses was only ever pushed to, never popped, so once a routine had returned its entry\nstayed on top — and Step Out aimed at an address the program would not reach again, running on\nto the next breakpoint instead. It went wrong whenever the routine you were in did not own the\nnewest entry: after a tail call (",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"jp SomeRoutine"}],", which pushes nothing, so the routine returns past\nits caller), and after stepping into and back out of a nested call before stepping out of the\nroutine containing it. 1cd:["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Follow a label to where it is defined."}]," A NEX bank’s disassembly context menu opens with ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"Go to\nDefinition"}]," whenever the line names a label — ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"ld hl,InitPalettes"}]," — and it takes you\nthere. 1ce:["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"A popped-out NEX bank shows the machine, not the file — without being asked."}]," Whenever a\nmachine is running, both the bank’s memory ",["$","em",null,{"children":"and"}]," its disassembly are built from the bank’s current\ncontents, with every byte that differs from the file marked and counted in the toolbar. This is how\nself-modifying code, a decompressed payload, or a bank corrupted by a stray write become visible\n— none of it can be told from a clean load otherwise. It works for every bank of the file,\nincluding ones the program has paged out, and falls back to the file’s bytes when no machine is\nrunning. There is no switch to find: a ",["$","code",null,{"className":"nextra-code","dir":"ltr","children":"Live"}]," marker in the toolbar says which of the two you are\nlooking at."]}]
1cf:["$","li",null,{"className":"x:my-[.5em]","children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"A paused NEX bank’s disassembly is aligned to the program counter."}]," Decoding a bank from its\nfirst byte is a guess about where instructions start, and self-modifying code or a jump table can\nmake it wrong for everything below. While the machine is paused with the PC inside the bank, the\nlisting is cut and re-decoded at the PC — the one offset where the alignment is known rather\nthan guessed — so the rows from there on are the instructions that will actually run. A\nlisting that was already aligned is left exactly as it was."]}]
